59 setts across 55 tartans with 28 stripes, grouped by colour-sequence pattern. Patterns with fewer than 40 setts are listed in full below; busier ones link to their own page. Earliest known date: 2002 The tartan for this Welsh surname and its variations, Williamson, Wills, Gwylim, is commercially accepted as a tartan or plaid in Wales, this is one of the tartans actually woven in Wales at the Cambrian Woollen Mill, weaving on the same site since 1830. This tartan differs from many traditional patterns in that the warp and weft differ, giving the finished worsted wool cloth more of a predominant stripe , vertically noticeable in the finished Kilt, or Cilt in Wales. James, his grandfather, had moved to The North Carolina Scottish Colony of the Cape Fear River from Scotland some time between 1745 and 1765 bringing the sett with him.
